### Feature

<!-- lore:019fb9b2-08da-79e4-8d1e-b7c75c70d7c6 -->
* **feat: restore Freestyle Cloud three-mode transcribe/cleanup**: Restored the three independent modes after #411 collapsed them: - Cloud transcription-only (voice=cloud, cleanup=off) - Cloud cleanup-only (voice=local, cleanup=cloud) - Combined (voice=cloud, cleanup=cloud) Server: restored \`CloudCleanupTones\`, \`appendCleanupFormFields()\`, \`mode\` param on \`transcribeWithFreestyleCloud()\`, \`postProcessWithFreestyleCloud()\`, \`/v2/post-process\` route, and \`Freestyle Cloud\` as a selectable LLM cleanup model. Streaming provider now returns \`mode:raw\` data. Renderer: pair-card collapses to unified view only when \*\*both\*\* sides are cloud; each side independently selectable otherwise. Tone page removed \`cloudVoiceActive\` short-circuit (wrong under three-mode). All plumbing (dictionaries, models, auth, settings) preserved. Verification: 212 server tests pass, electron + server typecheck clean, server build succeeds.

<!-- lore:019fb9aa-c24a-784e-bc8d-3425c8e78859 -->
* **Open model source button for proxy/TLS download failures**: Added 'Open model source' button for all proxy/TLS-class download failures (ProxyInterceptionError with sourceUrl or fallback HF URL). Server helper \`downloadErrorSourceUrl()\` in \`download-guard.ts\` returns the precise URL. State propagates via \`errorSourceUrl\` in \`ModelDownloadState\` through whisper/models.ts and mlx-asr/models.ts. Renderer shows button in model-list.tsx (row error + TierCard error) and model-setup-panel.tsx. i18n key \`onboarding.modelSetup.openModelSource\` added. Tests added in download-guard.test.ts. Button opens via \`window.api.openExternal()\` (preload already validates http/https).

### Fix

<!-- lore:019fb96f-4152-7275-95a7-18d4e55691de -->
* **Add initializing state handling in onHotkeyUp to prevent double-start**: When the hotkey is released while the pill is in the 'initializing' state (before mic access is granted), \`onHotkeyUp\` now calls \`preventDoubleStart()\` to ignore the release and avoid triggering a second concurrent recording attempt. This ensures the hold-to-record behavior remains correct during the initialization window.

<!-- lore:019fb96f-4140-7041-9f26-125ec8a148e5 -->
* **Linux CI: remove snap build target and fix artifact paths**: Removed \`snap\` from \`build.yml\` and \`electron-builder.yml\` to fix repeated \`FileNotFoundError\` in CI caused by missing \`@freestyle/\` subdirectory in dist. Changed \`artifactName\` from \`${name}\` to \`${productName}\` (and added \`appName\`/\`directories.output\`) so Linux outputs go to \`dist/Freestyle-\*.AppImage\` and \`dist/Freestyle-\*.deb\` without the invalid scoped subdirectory. This unblocks the Linux build while keeping AppImage and deb packaging intact.

<!-- lore:019fb96f-4148-71a5-a897-fdaabafe224e -->
* **Windows build: remove redundant typecheck step from build script**: Changed \`build:win\` script from \`npm run build\` (which ran \`typecheck\` and failed on pre-existing TS errors in \`typecheck:node\`) to \`electron-vite build\` directly, consistent with \`build:mac\` and \`build:linux\`. The shared typecheck step in CI (\`typecheck\`) remains as the gate, so this prevents spurious build failures unrelated to our changes.

### Gap

<!-- lore:019fb9c4-4a38-7803-ba58-86883820b740 -->
* **Plugin pipeline: missing error boundary in PluginPage**: PluginPage (plugin-page.tsx) renders PluginViewManager which can throw during render or lifecycle phases. There is no ErrorBoundary component wrapping the plugin UI, so plugin JS errors bubble up to the host app and can break the entire Electron window. Add an ErrorBoundary that catches plugin errors and renders a fallback UI with retry/uninstall options, and logs via captureException.

<!-- lore:019fb9c4-4a28-755e-b5e0-d8acb4f0d849 -->
* **Plugin pipeline: missing onUnmount cleanup hook**: The plugin pipeline defines hooks: event, config, beforeTranscribe, afterTranscribe, beforeCleanup, afterCleanup, beforeOutput. There is NO onUnmount hook defined. If a plugin needs to clean up resources when the plugin page unmounts (e.g., cancel timers, close WebWorkers, unsubscribe from streams), there is no canonical mechanism. The afterCleanup hook fires on the final text but not on UI unmount. This could cause resource leaks for plugins that allocate non-text resources. Add onUnmount to the Hooks interface and dispatch it when PluginPage unmounts in PluginViewManager.

<!-- lore:019fb9c4-4a41-7627-83e0-e333aafb599c -->
* **Plugin pipeline: missing plugin health/liveness monitoring**: PluginViewManager caches WebContentsView per slug/pageId but does not monitor whether the plugin's renderer process is still alive. If a plugin crashes or hangs, the cached view will show a frozen page and the host has no automatic recovery mechanism. Add a health ping (e.g., postMessage from plugin renderer) and a fallback UI that offers to reload the plugin page or report a crash.

<!-- lore:019fb9c4-4a49-765a-aca9-7f8da2a5e2f8 -->
* **Plugin pipeline: plugin CSS isolation / shadow DOM not enforced**: Plugin UI is rendered into a WebContentsView with no CSS encapsulation. Plugin styles can leak into the host app and vice versa, causing visual regressions. Consider using ShadowDOM or a scoped CSS strategy (e.g., CSS modules or a postload style reset) to isolate plugin styling. This is a medium-term gap.

### Gotcha

<!-- lore:019f2291-6849-7663-9bdf-441334b5371f -->
* **.lore.md uncommitted changes block branch checkout — commit on current branch first**: Trap: \`.lore.md\` is auto-updated by the lore system and frequently has uncommitted changes. When switching branches via \`gh pr checkout\` or \`git checkout\`, Git aborts if \`.lore.md\` differs between branches. The repo rules say NEVER stash \`.lore.md\` (it must stay version-controlled). Fix: commit the \`.lore.md\` change on the current branch first (\`git add .lore.md && git commit -m 'chore: update lore'\`), then switch branches. The commit is local-only until explicitly pushed. This is the correct resolution — stashing violates the lore rules.

<!-- lore:019fd64c-5ac7-7909-8a2b-87588aa54a48 -->
* **"Full screen" UI request may mean wider-but-capped, not edge-to-edge**: In apps/electron/src/renderer/src/shell.tsx:350, UpdateBanner's className controls width via \`w-\[calc(100%-3rem)]\` (1.5rem side gutters) plus a \`max-w-\*\` cap. Assistant's first implementation of a 'make updater take full screen' request removed \`max-w-2xl\` entirely, making the banner stretch to the full container width. User corrected this to \`max-w-5xl\` instead — 'full screen' meant noticeably wider but still capped, not truly edge-to-edge. Even after asking clarifying questions and getting an answer ('full-width banner'), confirm the exact numeric/visual intent before implementing width changes, since 'full width' is ambiguous between 'no cap' and 'a wider cap'.

<!-- lore:019fd03f-b22c-76c6-8a73-8af2b4a5e2a0 -->
* **@freestyle-voice/stt must be built via pkgroll before typecheck:node passes**: apps/electron's \`typecheck:node\` fails with TS2307 "Cannot find module '@freestyle-voice/stt'" across ~9 server files (llm/registry.ts, post-process.ts, remix-transform.ts, streaming providers, stream.ts, transcribe.ts) whenever the packages/stt workspace package hasn't been built yet. This looks like a real regression from whatever branch you're on, but it's just an unbuilt workspace dependency — fix by running packages/stt's build script (\`pkgroll --minify\`) once; unrelated to any specific PR's changes.

<!-- lore:019fd6b8-45d2-7601-b9e0-3f4d6d8a447b -->
* **@freestyle-voice/validations, not @freestyle/validations**: Trap: writing \`import { X } from "@freestyle/validations"\` looks correct because the monorepo's internal package is colloquially called 'validations', but the actual npm scope/package name is \`@freestyle-voice/validations\` (confirmed via existing imports like apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/history.tsx:1). Using the shortened scope causes a module-not-found error. Always copy the exact import path from an existing working file in the same directory rather than guessing the scope name.

<!-- lore:019fd6b5-cfc1-7785-8c7e-8f5f2c3bf7ee -->
* **@freestyle/validations is wrong scope — use @freestyle-voice/validations**: The validations package import scope is \`@freestyle-voice/validations\`, NOT \`@freestyle/validations\`. Writing \`@freestyle/validations\` looks correct (matches the shorter product name 'Freestyle') but fails to resolve. Confirmed correct scope via existing imports in apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/history.tsx:1. Always grep an existing working import of the target package before adding a new one rather than guessing the scope name.

<!-- lore:019fd6ea-fc19-7bfd-892d-532cb25babb6 -->
* **@freestyle/validations vs @freestyle-voice/validations alias split**: The shared validations package (packages/validations) is imported under different workspace aliases per repo: desktop (freestyle-voice/freestyle) uses \`@freestyle-voice/validations\`; cloud (freestyle-voice/cloud) uses \`@freestyle/validations\`. Same source package, different alias. Looks like a copy-paste-safe import when mirroring code (e.g. REMIX\_CLIENT\_TOOLS entries) between the two repos, but pasting the import statement unchanged resolves to nothing in the other repo. Always check the target repo's own import convention before mirroring an edit.

<!-- lore:019f657a-6225-7e48-b6e3-43bf01fe7cfb -->
* **@hono/node-server v2 WebSocket: must pass WebSocketServer to serve() explicitly**: The Network tab in Settings shows duplicate rows for \`network\_proxy\_url\` and \`network\_ca\_cert\_path\` because both the proxy/CA section and the legacy endpoint list each render their own GETs. The legacy endpoints (\`/api/settings/network\_proxy\_url\`, \`/api/settings/network\_ca\_cert\_path\`) were kept for backward compatibility and also queried by \`config.freestyle.json\` polling, causing 4–6 requests on page open. Consolidate by removing the legacy GET routes or deduplicating via a shared cache key; prefer the explicit proxy/CA section as the single source of truth.

<!-- lore:019f57ee-34b5-79e3-8a46-4bb376146ae4 -->
* **@hookform/resolvers@5 imports zod/v4/core but declares no zod peer dep — fix with pnpm packageExtensions**: Trap: \`@hookform/resolvers@5.4.0\` imports \`zod/v4/core\` internally but declares no zod peer dependency. Under pnpm isolated layout it resolves ambiently to a transitive \`zod@3.x\` which lacks the \`./v4/core\` export → Vite build fails with \`\[commonjs--resolver] Missing "./v4/core" specifier in "zod" package\`. Adding \`zod@^4\` as a direct devDep to the consuming package is insufficient — pnpm won't inject it into resolvers' node\_modules without an explicit peer declaration. Fix: add to \`pnpm-workspace.yaml\`: \`packageExtensions: { "@hookform/resolvers@\*": { dependencies: { zod: "^4.4.3" } } }\` then re-run \`pnpm install\`. This forces pnpm to symlink zod@4 into resolvers' own node\_modules. Confirmed fixed in PR \`9e1e376\`.

<!-- lore:019fd0c1-24a3-7279-8233-89dd48050ad4 -->
* **/api/settings fetched 3 separate uncached ways — cleanup-app-context.ts:45 hot path**: apps/electron renderer's \`/api/settings\` is fetched via 3 non-shared mechanisms: (1) cached \`\["settings-all"]\` query via \`settingsQueryOptions()\`, deduped across ~10 consumers with 1h staleTime; (2) a raw uncached \`getClient().api.settings.$get()\` at app.tsx mount (~line 2373); (3) \`refreshNeedsAppContextForCleanup()\` (cleanup-app-context.ts:45) calling the same raw endpoint on EVERY recording start via app.tsx:1522 (startRecording hot path). Looks fine because it's framed as a 'fresh read for a hot-path decision,' but the underlying data (llmCleanup + tone settings) rarely changes and is already fresh in the 1h cache. The codebase already has a sync variant, \`applyNeedsAppContextForCleanup(settings)\`, that reads from an already-fetched snapshot (used at app.tsx:2402) — prefer \`queryClient.getQueryData(\["settings-all"])\` over a network call in this hot path. history.tsx:445 similarly duplicates \`historyPaused\` via a separate \`\["setting", key]\` query instead of reading the settings-all map.

<!-- lore:019fd0cf-3bfd-70f8-a675-0f6827358145 -->
* **/api/settings fetched via 3 non-deduped paths in electron renderer**: apps/electron/src/renderer's getClient().api.settings.$get() is called 3 separate ways with no shared cache: (1) settingsQueryOptions() cached query key \["settings-all"] used by ~10 consumers; (2) raw uncached fetch at app.tsx mount (~line 2373); (3) raw uncached fetch via cleanup-app-context.ts's refreshNeedsAppContextForCleanup(), called from app.tsx startRecording on EVERY recording start (hot path) — fires a fresh network GET of the entire settings map on every hotkey press despite identical data being cached 1h in \["settings-all"]. Looks harmless since each call is 'just reading settings', but it triples the same fetch and adds needless latency to the hottest path. Fix: read via queryClient.getQueryData/ensureQueryData(\["settings-all"]) instead of a new network call. Found via Aug 2026 read-only audit; not yet fixed (audit was read-only, no code changed).

<!-- lore:019fcc32-c446-75ae-a86d-e21e785a9061 -->
* **actions/checkout@v6 pwn-request protection blocks pull\_request\_target fork checkout**: PR #541 CI failure: \`.github/workflows/changelog-preview.yml\` uses \`pull\_request\_target\` (runs with base-repo GITHUB\_TOKEN/secrets) and calls the reusable workflow \`getsentry/craft/.github/workflows/changelog-preview.yml@v2\`. That workflow's internal \`actions/checkout@v6\` refuses to check out fork PR code by default (new pwn-request vulnerability protection), erroring instead of skipping. Looks fixable via \`allow-unsafe-pr-checkout: true\`, but that flag lives inside Craft's reusable workflow — not settable from this repo without an upstream Craft change or inlining the workflow. Fix applied: add \`if: github.event.pull\_request.head.repo.full\_name == github.repository\` to the job so it's skipped (not failed) for external fork PRs — changelog preview is a maintainer convenience, not required for fork contributors to merge.

<!-- lore:019f2c4b-084a-7112-b7a1-832ec83fcff5 -->
* **After rebase adding new deps, run pnpm install before tests — missing package causes import failure**: Trap: after rebasing a branch that adds a new npm dependency (e.g. \`undici\`), tests fail with \`Cannot find package 'undici'\` — looks like a broken import or wrong package name. Fix: run \`pnpm install\` first; the lockfile update from the rebased commits isn't automatically applied to \`node\_modules\`. Observed in PR #388 rebase: 3 test files failed until \`pnpm install\` resolved the missing package in 3.8s, after which all 22 test files passed.

<!-- lore:019f4680-6d7b-7aba-bd5e-551122f532d9 -->
* **Always treat whisper/mlx status queries as stale (staleTime: 0): User directive (annotated in \`use-models.ts:162,177\`):**: Always treat whisper/mlx status queries as stale (staleTime: 0): User directive (annotated in \`use-models.ts:162,177\`): 'Status is volatile during downloads — always treat as stale.' Whisper and MLX model status queries must always use \`staleTime: 0\` — never cache these as fresh. Status changes rapidly during downloads and must always reflect the latest server state.

<!-- lore:019fc267-36a7-77f5-9634-85ea80032b7c -->
* **Android CI: assembleDebug/packageDebug hangs ~14min then fails IncrementalSplitterRunnable**: Trap: Android CI job "Mobile (Android build)" hangs for ~14 minutes with no log output after \`:sentry\_react-native:assembleDebug\` starts, then fails at \`:app:packageDebug\` with a generic \`IncrementalSplitterRunnable\` error and no \`Caused by\` trace — looks like a build/code issue, but the identical commit's PR run passed this same job. Suspected root cause: CI runner resource exhaustion (disk space or OOM) during APK packaging, not a code regression. Fix: re-run via \`gh run rerun --failed\`; consider adding a disk-cleanup step before the Android assembleDebug/packaging step (proposed, not yet implemented as of Aug 2026).

<!-- lore:019fcc2b-da19-7be6-baf2-ec687e5cf132 -->
* **app.tsx captureBarLines: barLinesRef never cleared on detach**: In apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/app.tsx, \`captureBarLines()\` sets \`barLinesRef.current\` on attach but deliberately never clears it on detach. This looks like a ref-cleanup bug (should null out on unmount) but is intentional: the waveform moves between two surfaces (capsule and remix card), and a detach firing after the new element's attach would blank the ref and freeze the visible bar row. Holding a stale detached element is harmless — the draw loop just writes attributes nobody renders until the next attach lands, so the bars never visibly jump when a surface takes over. Merge note (PR #534): main added a \`\[data-bars] line\` selector (bars wrapped in a sibling \`\<g data-bars="">\` next to the flatline) — when reconciling, keep main's selector but preserve remix's attach-only-never-clear behavior.

<!-- lore:019fb9e6-a61e-781f-b477-9a463c080e56 -->
* **apps/mobile cloud-fetch files use raw fetch(), no shared wrapper like cloudJson**: apps/mobile/src/lib/cloud/\*.ts (config.ts, preferences.ts, usage.ts, cloud-config.ts, profile.ts) implements its own \`cloudUrl()\` resolver (mirrors server's \`freestyleCloudUrl()\`) but has NO shared fetch wrapper equivalent to \`cloudJson()\` — every file calls raw \`fetch()\` directly with no retry/error-classification. This is an inconsistency vs. apps/server's centralized pattern, not an intentional design choice. Before adding new mobile cloud calls, check whether consolidating onto a shared wrapper is in scope rather than adding another one-off fetch.

<!-- lore:019fd28e-3b66-7314-b7b1-bd62627caf3c -->
* **apps/server DB layer: no indexes, no prepared-statement cache**: apps/server/src/lib/db.ts has zero CREATE INDEX statements in schema.ts and no prepared-statement cache — readSetting/writeSetting/deleteSetting and 100+ other call sites all call getDb().prepare(sql) inline, recompiling SQL every call. Missing indexes on transcription\_history(created\_at) (hit by history.ts, history-store.ts) and model\_configs(type, is\_default) (scanned twice per transcription in providers.ts) cause full table scans on hot paths. Also, per-dictation settings reads are ~25-35 individual point queries (getEffectiveCleanupTones = 6 readSetting calls, getLanguagesSetting = 2, getDefaultModels = 2, post-process.ts getters) instead of one batched \`SELECT key,value FROM settings WHERE key IN (...)\` (pattern already used in models.ts:54). Fix: add a Map\<sql,Statement> cache in db.ts, add the two indexes, and snapshot settings once per dictation.

<!-- lore:019fd0db-c2b7-717a-b90e-9814d964440b -->
* **areAllCleanupTonesOff gate skips cleanupAppAssignments capture**: cleanup-app-context.ts's applyNeedsAppContextForCleanup() and tone.tsx's CLEANUP\_CONTEXT\_KEYS only inspect SETTINGS\_KEYS.llmCleanup + the 4 base tones (personal/work/email/overall) via areAllCleanupTonesOff() — they never read cleanupAppAssignments (packages/validations/src/cleanup-tones.ts), which lets a user route a specific app/site into a tone group independent of the base tones. If all 4 base tones are 'off' but the user has app-assignment overrides configured, the frontmost-app capture is skipped entirely, so app assignments can never fire in that state. This is pre-existing behavior (not introduced by any recent changeset) — confirm product intent before treating as a bug. If assignments should always be able to fire, the gate needs to also check for non-empty cleanupAppAssignments.

<!-- lore:019fd228-c3ff-7b32-b221-efb6984566f7 -->
* **auth-context loading gate can stall dashboard mount up to ~15s on cold server**: \`auth-context.tsx\` starts \`loading=true\` and only clears it once \`refreshInternal()\` succeeds — but the retry loop (lines 91-97) attempts up to 15 times with 1s backoff waiting for \`/api/auth/status\` to respond (\`reached===true\`, line 63) before calling \`setLoading(false)\` (line 97). Looks like a quick reachability check but on a cold/slow local server this can delay \`AppShell\`/\`HistoryPage\` mount — and therefore every query inside it (settings, plugins, history, stats, daily, dismissed-notifications) — by up to ~15s, since none of those queries mount until \`LoginGate\`'s \`loading\` gate clears. Concurrent calls (mount + window-focus listener) are deduped via \`refreshInFlightRef\` so this isn't a double-fetch bug, just a genuine serial blocking dependency worth checking first when diagnosing slow first paint on /today.

<!-- lore:019fd1f7-9bb7-7bcc-a4ca-e2808890baf6 -->
* **auth-context.tsx fires /api/auth/status twice at dashboard mount**: CloudAuthProvider (apps/electron renderer lib/auth-context.tsx) has exactly one call site for /api/auth/status (line 50, refreshInternal()), but two effects invoke it at mount: (1) the initial reachability retry-loop effect (lines 72-85) fires it immediately; (2) the revalidation effect's window 'focus' listener (lines 87-91) fires it again because the main process calls settingsWindow.show()+.focus() right after creating/loading the window (index.ts:1007-1008, 1869-1870), which triggers a focus event during mount. In dev, React StrictMode double-invoking effects can add a further hit. Not a bug per se — it's the byproduct of a reachability-retry-on-mount + focus-revalidation design — but explains the 'x2' seen in mount-time network logs.

<!-- lore:019fd20b-61ff-7e66-a035-f3e40fcdaa60 -->
* **auth-context.tsx retry loop can delay dashboard mount up to ~15s**: \`auth-context.tsx\` starts \`loading=true\` and its retry loop (lines 91-97) calls \`await refreshInternal()\` up to 15 attempts with 1s backoff, only calling \`setLoading(false)\` once \`reached===true\` (line 63, when \`/api/auth/status\` returns any response). Against a cold/slow local server this can hold the entire dashboard content subtree (login-gate.tsx gates rendering on \`loading\`) unmounted for up to ~15 seconds, showing only a blank \`AuthLoadingFrame\`. Looks like a normal auth check but is actually an unbounded-looking retry against local server startup latency. Concurrent calls (mount-time + window \`focus\` listener) are deduped via \`refreshInFlightRef\` (lines 47-56, 76-81) so they don't double-hit \`/api/auth/status\`.

<!-- lore:019f2e88-285e-7702-95ca-95863e35b158 -->
* **autoUpdater.checkForUpdatesAndNotify() leaks downloadPromise rejection — use runUpdateCheck() instead**: Trap: \`checkForUpdatesAndNotify()\` looks like the canonical high-level API for update checks. But with \`autoDownload: true\`, it internally drops \`downloadPromise\` in a \`void ...then()\` with no \`.catch()\` (electron-updater's \`AppUpdater.js\`), so transient CDN failures (e.g. expired 403 on release asset) leak as unhandled rejections and get reported as false crashes. Fix: use \`runUpdateCheck()\` (defined in \`index.ts\`) which calls \`checkForUpdates()\` directly, swallows \`result?.downloadPromise?.catch(() => {})\`, and logs check failures via \`log.warn\`. The \`update-downloaded\` handler already shows the completion notification, making \`checkForUpdatesAndNotify()\` redundant.

<!-- lore:019fcc2b-da21-76f5-b99f-6731dcee4b38 -->
* **BarMode 'connecting' renamed to 'listening' in app.tsx**: The \`BarMode\` union value \`"connecting"\` was renamed to \`"listening"\` on main (apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/app.tsx). Old code paths calling \`startBarAnimation("connecting")\` (e.g. remix's mic-failure fallback for idle bars) must be updated to \`"listening"\` — main's \`listening\` mode renders the same flat/idle bar row intent ("pill is awake but has nothing to show yet"). Searching for \`"connecting"\` as a BarMode value will not find current code; search for \`listening\` instead.

<!-- lore:019fcbf0-d163-7701-9b83-34ddda747313 -->
* **BarMode value renamed "connecting" → "listening" (main branch)**: Main renamed the \`BarMode\` value \`"connecting"\` → \`"listening"\` in apps/electron's pill animation code (\`startBarAnimation\`), changing semantics from a slow breathing 'awaiting audio' animation to a flat/idle bar row. Callers still passing \`"connecting"\` (e.g. remix-prototype's mic-failure fallback) fail with TS2345. When merging/rebasing older branches, remap \`"connecting"\` calls to \`"listening"\` — the flat-idle rendering is intentional ("idle bars so the card doesn't look broken"), not a bug.

<!-- lore:019faf4f-7e28-7e9a-8555-40415c3c1b24 -->
* **Better Auth unlinkAccount: lockout protection is server-only, add explicit UI guard too**: authClient.unlinkAccount({ providerId }) is built into better-auth and by default blocks unlinking the user's last linked account (prevents lockout) unless the server sets allowUnlinkingAll:true. It can target a specific accountId when multiple accounts share a provider. Also note: linking a provider with updateUserInfoOnLink can copy profile fields (name, image) but never changes the user's email/emailVerified — linking cannot rebind account identity. Trap: relying on server-side lockout protection alone gives no UX feedback — always add an explicit client-side guard (disable the 'Disconnect' button + tooltip) when it's the user's only sign-in method, matching the pattern used in apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/profile.tsx.

<!-- lore:019f40a9-8d1a-729b-a5e8-9956cd182333 -->
* **Biome noLabelWithoutControl fires on \<label> wrapping Radix Switch — use \<div> instead**: Trap: wrapping a Radix \`Switch\` in a \`\<label>\` element looks correct for accessibility (label → control association). Biome's \`noLabelWithoutControl\` rule fires because the \`\<label>\` has no \`htmlFor\` and Radix Switch renders its own internal label handling. Fix: replace \`\<label>\` with \`\<div>\` — Radix Switch manages its own interaction and accessibility without needing an outer \`\<label>\`.

<!-- lore:019f1407-8c5e-7fe0-8bb6-1323c31da778 -->
* **buildLanguageBlock() returned empty string for auto-detect — LLM translated non-English speech to English**: Trap: \`buildLanguageBlock(undefined)\` and \`buildLanguageBlock('auto')\` both returned \`''\`, so the LLM cleanup prompt had no 'do not translate' constraint for auto-detect sessions. This looks safe because the preset body already contains a weaker 'Keep the output in the same language' rule — but that rule is buried and insufficient to override English-biased LLM behavior. Fix (commit 5db8118): \`buildLanguageBlock()\` now returns \`AUTO\_LANGUAGE\_CONSTRAINT\` for both \`undefined\`/empty and \`'auto'\` cases, explicitly instructing the LLM to preserve source language and script. \`prompts.test.ts\` updated: test renamed from 'returns nothing for auto-detect' to assert the constraint is present.

<!-- lore:019f6b7b-1b9f-73c0-8e6d-b85c752fc396 -->
* **buildPluginLlm() throws on freestyle-cloud provider — expected, not a bug**: Trap: \`buildPluginLlm()\` in \`apps/server/src/lib/plugins/llm.ts:19\` calls \`createChatModel(llm.provider, llm.model\_id)\` on every dictation via \`createHookApi()\`. When cleanup provider is \`freestyle-cloud\`, \`providers.ts:60\` throws \`Unsupported provider: freestyle-cloud\` because Freestyle Cloud is a remote HTTP endpoint, not a locally-instantiable AI SDK chat model. Error is caught, logged at debug level, returns \`undefined\`. This is expected behavior — \`freestyle-cloud\` is intentionally absent from \`registry.ts\` (only openai, groq, anthropic, google, mistral, local-llm). The debug log \`plugin LLM capability unavailable: Unsupported provider: freestyle-cloud\` appears ~twice per streaming dictation (once per \`createHookApi()\` call). Do NOT suppress this log — it is intentional signal. Plugins guard with \`if (ctx.llm)\` and must not assume LLM capability is present.

<!-- lore:019fd060-98ef-7ff6-b645-3ef02d6c07c2 -->
* **buildRemixAgentSystem must gate web\_search/image\_search via hasWebSearch capability flag**: Trap: REMIX\_AGENT\_PROMPT unconditionally instructed the model to call web\_search/image\_search for facts, and it looked safe because the prompt is deliberately "mirrored byte-for-byte" across cloud and BYOK for parity. But parity of the PROMPT TEXT doesn't imply parity of TOOL AVAILABILITY — web\_search/image\_search are Freestyle Cloud server-only tools; the renderer's client-side dispatcher (\`executeTool()\` in remix-chat.tsx) has no case for them, and BYOK's \`runRemixAgentLocally\` only registers \`remixClientTools()\`. Result: BYOK runs following the prompt hit \`{ok:false, reason:"unknown tool: web\_search"}\`, burning a step of the 16-step budget then recovering from memory (contradicting the prompt's own "never edit from memory of a stale snapshot" rule). Fix (PR #546): \`buildRemixAgentSystem(context, capabilities)\` now takes \`RemixAgentCapabilities({hasWebSearch})\`; cloud passes \`true\` (byte-identical output preserved), BYOK passes \`false\`, stripping the GATHER web\_search clause, the \`## Search\` section, and the image-insert recipe.

<!-- lore:019fd679-d7ab-75c6-8552-37dd9bedf816 -->
* **buildVoiceRows ignores EndpointConnectState.models — custom endpoint results unselectable**: In apps/electron .../models/model-list.tsx, \`buildVoiceRows()\` builds voice picker rows purely from \`m.voiceItems\` (derived from \`buildVoiceItems\`/\`buildSettingsVoiceItems\` over \`AvailableModel\[]\`). The custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint flow (\`useEndpointConnect\` → \`runEndpointTest\` in use-endpoint-connect.ts) discovers models via a \`/v1/models\` probe into \`EndpointConnectState.models: string\[]\`, but that array is never merged into \`voiceItems\`. Looks fine because \`EndpointConnectForm\` shows 'Connected · N models found' — appears successful — but no code path turns those IDs into selectable \`Row\`s. Users can configure the endpoint but can't pick a specific model; the active voice model silently stays whatever it was (e.g. \`openai/whisper-1\`), just re-routed to the new base URL. Fix in progress (Aug 2026): render discovered models as selectable rows that configure \`openai/\<model-id>\`.

<!-- lore:019f65b0-dcea-7ed4-bfb3-77379861925f -->
* **cancelRecording never calls streamerRef.current?.cancel() — server-side stream left open**: Trap: \`cancelRecording\` in \`app.tsx\` (lines 1031–1037) handles the batch path cleanup but never calls \`streamerRef.current?.cancel()\`. Looks complete because it stops the MediaRecorder and releases the stream. Fix: add \`streamerRef.current?.cancel()\` inside \`cancelRecording\` so the server-side WebSocket/streaming session is torn down on cancel, not left open until timeout.

<!-- lore:019fcc60-20e1-7ef5-9297-bf14c873403e -->
* **captureBarLines must not clear barLinesRef on detach**: In apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/app.tsx, the ref callback \`captureBarLines\` (attached to the SVG \`\<line>\` bar elements, selector \`\[data-bars] line\`) must only \*set\* \`barLinesRef.current\` on attach — never clear it to null/\[] on detach. The waveform moves between two surfaces (pill capsule and remix card); if detach fires after the new element's attach, clearing the ref blanks it and freezes the visible bar row. Holding a stale/detached element is harmless — the draw loop just writes attributes nobody renders until the next attach lands. This looks like a memory-leak risk (stale DOM ref) but the fix (clearing on detach, which main's version did) actually breaks the cross-surface handoff. Recurred across 20+ sessions touching this component.

<!-- lore:019fcc55-0737-750f-80c1-a03ea29e5a1b -->
* **captureBarLines never clears barLinesRef on detach (waveform surface handoff)**: In app.tsx, \`barLinesRef.current\` is set on attach but deliberately never cleared on detach. This looks like a leak/bug because you'd expect refs to null out on unmount, but the waveform moves between two surfaces (capsule and remix card), and clearing on a detach that fires after the new element's attach would blank the visible row and freeze it. Holding a stale/detached element is harmless — the draw loop writes attributes that nobody renders until the next attach lands. When merging branches that touch this selector, combine main's broader \`\[data-bars] line\` selector (needed because main wraps bars in a sibling \`\<g data-bars>\`) with this never-clear-on-detach behavior rather than reintroducing a clear-on-null-svg branch.

<!-- lore:019fcbf0-d15d-743a-921f-a13ddac0c33e -->
* **captureBarLines: never clear ref on detach (waveform handoff capsule↔remix-card)**: In app.tsx's \`captureBarLines\`, whether to clear \`barLinesRef.current\` on detach is a deliberate design point, not an oversight. Remix's version only \*sets\* the ref on attach and never clears on detach, because the waveform SVG moves between two surfaces (capsule and remix card) — clearing on detach could blank the ref and freeze the visible row if the old surface detaches after the new one attaches. Main's version cleared to \`\[]\` when svg is null, which looks safer but breaks the capsule↔remix-card handoff. Merged resolution: use main's \`\[data-bars] line\` selector (needed for main's \`\<g data-bars="">\` wrapper) but keep remix's never-clear-on-detach behavior. Any refactor of bar-line capture must not add clear-on-detach.

<!-- lore:019fb9ae-8437-79a1-a62d-782187096753 -->
* **CGEvent tap suppresses compound hotkey KEY\_DOWN events**: The CGEvent tap that suppresses compound hotkeys (to prevent dead-key insertion) also swallows the key event before NSEvent.addGlobalMonitorForEvents(.keyDown) sees it. That monitor is the only source of KEY\_DOWN:\<name> events emitted to Electron. Result: suppressed hotkeys never trigger dictation. Fix: emit KEY\_DOWN:\<name> from the CGEvent tap before returning nil for suppressed events. No duplicate risk — non-suppressed events are handled by the NSEvent monitor as before. Confirmed via KeyCastr showing only ⌥ when hotkey is pressed.

<!-- lore:019fcbfb-ee5a-7137-b3f7-573040580d17 -->
* **changelog-preview.yml fails on fork PRs — checkout@v6 pwn-request protection**: \`.github/workflows/changelog-preview.yml\` uses \`pull\_request\_target\` and calls Craft's reusable workflow (\`getsentry/craft/.github/workflows/changelog-preview.yml@v2\`) with \`secrets: inherit\`. Craft's internal \`actions/checkout@v6\` step refuses to check out fork PR code under \`pull\_request\_target\` (pwn-request protection) — looks like a repo misconfig but is intentional: pull\_request\_target runs with base-repo secrets/token, so checking out untrusted fork code would leak them. The opt-out flag \`allow-unsafe-pr-checkout: true\` lives inside Craft's reusable workflow, NOT settable from this repo, so it can't be fixed locally without an upstream Craft change or inlining the workflow. Fix applied: added \`if: github.event.pull\_request.head.repo.full\_name == github.repository\` to the job so it's skipped (not failed) for external fork PRs — changelog preview is a maintainer convenience, not required for merging.

<!-- lore:019fcbd7-f7ae-7104-82ff-70aa9e49e0cf -->
* **changelog-preview.yml pull\_request\_target blocks fork PR checkout (actions/checkout@v6)**: \`.github/workflows/changelog-preview.yml\` uses \`pull\_request\_target\` (runs with base repo's GITHUB\_TOKEN/secrets) and delegates to reusable workflow \`getsentry/craft/.github/workflows/changelog-preview.yml@v2\`. That workflow's internal \`actions/checkout@v6\` refuses to check out fork PR code by default (pwn-request protection) unless \`allow-unsafe-pr-checkout: true\` is set — but that flag lives inside Craft's reusable workflow, not settable from this repo, so it can't be opted into without an upstream Craft change. Fix applied: added an \`if\` guard comparing \`github.event.pull\_request.head.repo.full\_name != github.repository\` to skip the job entirely for external fork PRs (shows as 'skipped', not 'failed', so doesn't block merge). Changelog preview is a maintainer convenience only — fork contributors don't need it.

<!-- lore:019fd70e-bf6d-73e6-ac9a-c1298133eeb6 -->
* **Cleanup intensity has no 'off' — llm\_cleanup toggle is separate**: cleanupIntensitySchema = z.enum(\["low","medium","high","custom"]) (packages/validations/src/settings.ts) deliberately excludes "off". Enabling/disabling cleanup entirely is a separate setting, llm\_cleanup, exposed on the Models page, not the Tone page. This is asymmetric with per-destination tones (personal/work/email/overall in packages/validations/src/cleanup-tones.ts), which each DO include "off" — meaning skip the destination-specific tone block while the base preset still runs. Don't assume intensity's absence-of-value or "custom" means cleanup is disabled; check llm\_cleanup separately.

<!-- lore:019fd6e5-2d15-7be0-a780-1c7b3f31a03a -->
* **cleanup\_intensity enum has no 'off' value**: \`cleanupIntensitySchema\` (packages/validations/src/settings.ts) is \`z.enum(\["low","medium","high","custom"])\` — no "off" option, even though every per-destination tone enum (personal/work/email/overall) DOES include "off". Looks inconsistent/like a missing case, but is intentional: cleanup on/off is a separate concern controlled by the \`llm\_cleanup\` setting (Models page), not by intensity. Don't add an "off" value to intensity or assume intensity controls whether cleanup runs at all — check \`llm\_cleanup\` instead.

<!-- lore:019fd6e6-ad9e-78bc-8229-1cf71ad1bfa8 -->
* **cleanup\_intensity has no 'off' value — distinct from per-destination tone 'off'**: cleanupIntensitySchema (packages/validations/src/settings.ts) is enum low|medium|high|custom — there is no "off" option. It looks like intensity should support disabling cleanup, but that's a separate setting entirely: llm\_cleanup (set on the Models page) controls whether cleanup runs at all. By contrast, per-destination tones (personal/work/email/overall, cleanup-tones.ts) each DO have an "off" value, meaning "run the base preset only, skip the destination tone block" — NOT "skip cleanup". Don't conflate the two: intensity off doesn't exist, and destination-tone off ≠ disabling cleanup.

<!-- lore:019fd6c6-ce0f-7a9e-bb0f-d0cc474b2c9d -->
* **cleanup\_intensity has no 'off' value — enablement is llm\_cleanup, not intensity**: cleanupIntensitySchema (packages/validations/src/settings.ts) is z.enum(\["low","medium","high","custom"]) — there is no "off" intensity, even though it looks like it should double as an enable/disable switch. Cleanup enablement is a wholly separate setting: llm\_cleanup (toggled on the Models page). Per-destination tones (cleanup\_personal\_tone/work\_tone/email\_tone/overall\_tone) DO have "off", but that means the base cleanup preset still runs with no destination-specific tone/structure block applied — not that cleanup is disabled. Don't conflate the two "off" semantics. areAllCleanupTonesOff() only decides whether app-context capture is needed, not whether cleanup runs.

<!-- lore:019fd0c8-1af1-7954-b12b-6f9a6efa98f3 -->
* **cleanup-app-context.ts bypasses settings-all TanStack Query cache**: refreshNeedsAppContextForCleanup() (apps/electron/src/renderer/src/lib/cleanup-app-context.ts:45) calls getClient().api.settings.$get() directly — a raw, uncached GET of /api/settings — invoked from app.tsx startRecording (line ~1522) on EVERY recording start (hotkey press = hot path). This duplicates the already-cached \["settings-all"] query (settingsQueryOptions(), staleTime 1h, ~10 consumers). It looks safe because a sibling sync function, applyNeedsAppContextForCleanup(settings), already derives the same value from an in-memory snapshot — but startRecording calls the network variant instead of feeding it from the query cache. A second raw uncached /api/settings fetch also happens at app.tsx mount (~line 2373). Fix: read via queryClient.getQueryData/ensureQueryData(\["settings-all"]) instead of firing a fresh network call on every recording start.

<!-- lore:019fd0db-c290-76a4-85cd-6cb4bad81e23 -->
* **cleanup-app-context.ts cache requires IPC fire on every llm\_cleanup/tone writer**: Pill's app.tsx uses synchronous getNeedsAppContextForCleanup() (apps/electron/.../lib/cleanup-app-context.ts) instead of an async /api/settings fetch on every recording, avoiding a settings round-trip in the hot path — a concrete instance of the pill's own-cache pattern \[\[019fd0cb-e476-7236-816a-9f9f22be7cd0]]. Cache defaults to true (fail-open) until primed at pill mount inside a .then() of an initial settings fetch (not guaranteed complete before the first recording — cold start may trigger one extra unnecessary frontmost-app capture; deemed safe). Kept fresh via IPC \`settings:cleanup-context-changed\`, sent by every writer of SETTINGS\_KEYS.llmCleanup or the 4 cleanup tone keys (personal/work/email/overall) — tone.tsx's CLEANUP\_CONTEXT\_KEYS and use-models.ts's setCleanup are today's only writers. Any NEW write path to these keys must also call window.api.sendCleanupContextChanged(), or the pill's cached routing decision goes silently stale — there's no compile-time enforcement of this.

<!-- lore:019fd0fd-5e4c-77da-8cbf-8f3e17a618dd -->
* **cleanup-app-context.ts hot-path refetch fixed via cleanup-context-changed IPC**: startRecording() (app.tsx) called refreshNeedsAppContextForCleanup() (cleanup-app-context.ts) on every hotkey press, firing a raw uncached GET /api/settings even though identical data was already cached 1h in React Query under \["settings-all"]. Looked harmless since it's fire-and-forget/non-blocking, but it added needless network latency/load to the hot path. Fix: since the pill has no QueryClientProvider \[\[019fd0cb-e476-7236-816a-9f9f22be7cd0]] and can't read the dashboard's cache directly, added a synchronous getNeedsAppContextForCleanup() getter (returns cached bool, defaults to true fail-open) for the hot path, plus a new "cleanup-context-changed" IPC broadcast (sendCleanupContextChanged/onCleanupContextChanged, following the sendXChanged/onXChanged convention) fired from every writer of llmCleanup/cleanup-tone settings (tone.tsx saveSetting, use-models.ts setCleanup) so the pill's cache refreshes only on real settings changes, not every recording.

<!-- lore:019fd0b5-25cd-7c10-b2d8-8d166ae8d10b -->
* **cleanup-app-context.ts triplicates /api/settings fetch on every recording start**: apps/electron renderer's refreshNeedsAppContextForCleanup() (lib/cleanup-app-context.ts:45) calls getClient().api.settings.$get() directly, bypassing the cached \["settings-all"] TanStack Query (settingsQueryOptions(), 1h staleTime). It's invoked from app.tsx's startRecording on EVERY recording start (hotkey-press hot path); app.tsx also does a separate raw settings fetch at mount (~line 2373). Looks safe because a sync sibling, applyNeedsAppContextForCleanup(settings), already derives the same flag from an existing snapshot with no network call — but startRecording calls the network-fetching variant instead of reading queryClient.getQueryData(\["settings-all"]). Net effect: /api/settings is fetched via 3 separate uncoordinated paths instead of one shared cache. Fix (not applied — found during a read-only audit): make refreshNeedsAppContextForCleanup read from the QueryClient cache via getQueryData/ensureQueryData.

<!-- lore:019fd0d9-70e1-7e0e-bee1-a984c8a7ad41 -->
* **cleanupAppAssignments never fire when all base cleanup tones are 'off'**: \`areAllCleanupTonesOff(tones)\` (packages/validations/src/cleanup-tones.ts) returns true only when personal/work/email/overall tones are ALL "off", and this gates whether the pill captures the frontmost app (via \`applyNeedsAppContextForCleanup\`/\`CLEANUP\_CONTEXT\_KEYS\` in cleanup-app-context.ts and tone.tsx). It looks correct because 'no tones enabled' implies 'no cleanup routing needed' — but it ignores \`cleanupAppAssignments\`, user-defined per-app/per-site tone overrides that are meant to be consulted independently of the base tones. Result: if a user sets all base tones off but still has app-specific overrides configured, frontmost-app capture is skipped entirely and those overrides can never fire. This is pre-existing behavior (not introduced by any recent changeset) and was flagged during code review for intent confirmation — not yet resolved.

<!-- lore:019fd6e8-06e0-78f9-b08a-0c72ccd9ac10 -->
* **cleanupIntensitySchema has no 'off' — llm\_cleanup gates enablement**: \`cleanupIntensitySchema\` (packages/validations/src/settings.ts) is \`low|medium|high|custom\` — there is no "off" value. This looks inconsistent because every per-destination tone enum (cleanupPersonalTone/Work/Email/Overall in cleanup-tones.ts) DOES include "off" and defaults to it, so it's tempting to assume intensity gates cleanup the same way. It doesn't: whether cleanup LLM runs at all is controlled by a separate boolean setting \`llm\_cleanup\` (set on the Models page), independent of intensity. Don't add an "off" intensity value or branch on intensity to decide if cleanup runs — check \`llm\_cleanup\` instead.

<!-- lore:019fd744-4272-71ec-bdb0-665a75f8e761 -->
* **cleanupIntensitySchema has no 'off' value — use llm\_cleanup toggle instead**: cleanupIntensitySchema (packages/validations/src/settings.ts) is low|medium|high|custom — deliberately NO 'off' value, unlike the four per-destination tone schemas in cleanup-tones.ts (personal/work/email/overall) which all include 'off' and default to it. This inconsistency looks like a missing enum member, but cleanup enablement is a SEPARATE boolean setting (\`llm\_cleanup\`, set on the Models page, checked via \`isLlmCleanupEnabled()\` in post-process.ts) — intensity only controls prompt strength once cleanup is already enabled. Don't add an 'off' branch to cleanupIntensitySchema to represent disablement.

<!-- lore:019f6ad6-921f-7224-a369-9de486f1ea85 -->
* **Client: fire pre-warm on record start**: In \`apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/app.tsx\`, \`startRecording\` now fires a fire-and-forget \`POST /api/transcribe/pre-warm\` immediately after requesting mic access. This warms whisper or MLX in parallel with user speaking, reducing first-submit latency. Server decides whether warming is needed (no-op for cloud/BYOK).

<!-- lore:019fc2e5-f0fe-7f9a-a966-29b6ee26ba25 -->
* **Cloud DO translate guard uses post-fallback resolved languages**: In the Freestyle Cloud streaming Durable Object (stt-session.ts), \`translate\` is enabled only when \`this.languages.length === 1\`, where \`this.languages\` is the POST-FALLBACK resolved list (payload languages ?? stored member\_preferences languages), not the raw request payload. This lets desktop/mobile drop \`languages\` from the streaming \`start\` message while still sending \`translate\` unconditionally from the local setting — the DO's fallback still resolves to a single language when appropriate. Gotchas: (1) if the stored language is the Hinglish alias "hi-en", \`normalizeLanguages\` expands it to \["hi","en"] (length 2), silently disabling translate; (2) if the D1 read of stored prefs fails, it fails open to null, making \`this.languages\` undefined and disabling translate with no error surfaced.

<!-- lore:019fc2ee-7516-7e07-81b5-0245bc10d617 -->
* **Cloud v2 schema asymmetry: customPrompt .optional() vs appContext .nullish()**: Freestyle Cloud's v2 schemas mix \`.optional()\` and \`.nullish()\` inconsistently. \`postProcessV2BodySchema.customPrompt\` is \`.optional()\` only — sending \`null\` fails validation, so callers must use \`value || undefined\`, never \`?? null\`. \`appContext\` is \`.nullish()\` and accepts \`null\` fine — using \`||\` there would be wrong too since empty string is valid. Also \`/v2/transcribe\` receives \`appAssignments\` as a JSON-encoded string (multipart form), while \`/v2/post-process\` receives the same field as a real array (JSON body) — same logical field, different wire encoding per endpoint. This is flagged in code as 'a recurring source of payload bugs' — always check the exact per-field schema modifier before choosing \`|| undefined\` vs \`?? null\` when building cloud payloads.

<!-- lore:019fc2e5-f10e-7f82-83d7-f275caaa6ef5 -->
* **Cloud v2 transcribe vs post-process payload encoding asymmetry**: Freestyle Cloud's two v2 endpoints encode the same logical fields differently: \`/v2/transcribe\` is multipart FormData, so \`appAssignments\`/\`languages\`/\`vocabulary\` must be JSON-encoded STRINGS (parsed server-side via parseCleanupAppAssignments etc.); \`/v2/post-process\` is native JSON, so these fields arrive as real arrays/objects — no string parsing. Also \`customPrompt\` on post-process must be passed as \`|| undefined\` (not \`?? null\`) because \`postProcessV2BodySchema\` uses \`.optional()\` not \`.nullish()\`, while \`appContext\` on the same schema uses \`.nullish()\` and does accept null. This mismatch looks like it should be uniform since both endpoints serve the same cleanup config, but mixing conventions (sending null customPrompt, forgetting to JSON.stringify appAssignments for transcribe) is documented in cloud-repo lore as 'a recurring source of payload bugs.' Always check the specific v2 Zod schema before adding/removing a field on either endpoint's payload builder.

<!-- lore:019f1507-12d2-751f-9992-27c7faca6415 -->
* **cloudJson throws plain Error with no status — cloud 5xx silently captured as app defect**: \`cloudJson\` in \`freestyle-cloud.ts\` now throws \`FreestyleCloudRequestError(status, detail)\` (not plain \`Error\`) on non-OK non-401 non-429 responses. \`FreestyleCloudUsageError\` (429) is never reported as an app defect. \`FreestyleCloudRequestError\` (5xx) and transient network errors are never captured as app defects — callers gate \`captureException\` with \`isTransientCloudError(err)\`. \`isTransientCloudError\` walks \`.cause\` chain via seen-set loop; returns true for \`FreestyleCloudRequestError\` with \`status >= 500\`, any node with a code in \`TRANSIENT\_NETWORK\_CODES\`, or \`name === 'TimeoutError'/'AbortError'\`. \`RETRIABLE\_CONNECTION\_CODES\` is a narrower subset (excludes response-phase timeouts) used by \`isRetriableConnectionError\` for the retry loop. \`cloudJson\` retries up to \`CLOUD\_FETCH\_ATTEMPTS=2\` times with \`CLOUD\_RETRY\_DELAY\_MS=150\` on retriable connection errors only.

<!-- lore:019fd6a4-7644-77fe-8839-92bc3ff12cc3 -->
* **CloudStep Continue button only renders when signed in — no prod skip path**: Onboarding's CloudStep (onboarding.tsx:1125-1198, the first screen) has NO 'Continue' button when signed out — only SignInButton ('Sign in via browser') and a dev/E2E-only 'Skip for now (dev)' ghost button, shown only when \`!user && (import.meta.env.DEV || window.api?.isE2E)\`. The Continue button (line 1188) only renders once \`user\` is truthy. This is intentional: browser sign-in is the sole way past onboarding in production; skip is a local-dev/E2E affordance only. Don't assume onboarding is bypassable by clicking through in a production build.

<!-- lore:019f1697-9d41-71a8-8c6a-4b0158de1365 -->
* **commitRecording restore guard must use duckingPromiseRef, not \_audioPlaybackMode**: Trap: guarding \`restoreSystemAudioSafely()\` in \`commitRecording\` with \`if (\_audioPlaybackMode !== 'off')\` looks correct — why restore if ducking is disabled? But \`\_audioPlaybackMode\` is mutable module state; if the user toggles duck/pause → off \*during\* an active recording, the commit skips restore and strands system volume at ~15% (\`DUCKED\_VOLUME\`). Fix: gate on \`duckingPromiseRef.current\` instead — truthy only when this specific session actually started ducking. \`restoreSystemAudioSafely\` already awaits the ref before calling IPC restore, so the guard is simply \`if (duckingPromiseRef.current)\`. Fixed in commit \`eaf9c07\`.

<!-- lore:019f6a4a-7d22-74c9-8cdf-3770614bbfbc -->
* **contextBridge strips Response prototype getters — plugin bridge api() must return plain object**: Trap: \`plugin-bridge.ts\` returning \`fetch(path, init)\` directly looks correct — the \`Response\` is a valid object. But \`contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld\` serializes only own enumerable properties; \`Response.ok\`, \`.status\`, \`.statusText\` are prototype getters, so the serialized result is \`{}\`. Plugin UI code sees \`res.ok === undefined\` (falsy) and throws \`'server returned undefined'\`. Fix: eagerly consume the body in the preload world and return a plain serializable object with \`ok\`, \`status\`, \`statusText\`, \`headers\`, and function properties \`json()\`/\`text()\`/\`arrayBuffer()\` that decode the pre-read \`ArrayBuffer\`. \`contextBridge\` wraps functions as proxies so callable methods in the returned object survive the boundary. Affects all plugin UI pages using \`window.freestyle.api()\`.

<!-- lore:019efae2-43a2-72d9-b1d1-b36349845d80 -->
* **Craft npm workspace mode bumps ALL packages to same version — cannot use for independent versioning**: Trap: Craft's npm target supports \`workspaces: true\` with \`includeWorkspaces\`/\`excludeWorkspaces\` filters, which looks like it enables per-package independent versioning from a single root \`.craft.yml\`. Fix: workspace mode still publishes all included packages at the SAME version under a single release pipeline. For truly independent versions, each package needs its own \`.craft.yml\` in its subdirectory and a separate workflow using the \`path\` input. Craft source even has a \`// TODO support multiple configuration files\` comment confirming this is a known limitation.

<!-- lore:019fcbe2-8b7b-7ec5-91bb-a5c414d9a000 -->
* **CSS enter transition doesn't fire when data-show is true at mount**: Trap: a component mounted by React with a CSS-transition-driving attribute (e.g. \`data-show="true"\`) already set on first paint looks like it should animate in — but CSS transitions only fire on a \*change\* to an attribute/property, never on its initial value. Fix (used for the voice pill in apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/app.tsx): mount with the attribute false/absent, hold for one committed frame, then flip it to true to trigger the transition. Applies to any data-attribute-driven CSS transition in this codebase, not just the pill. Fixed in PR #541 (voice-pill-motion).

<!-- lore:019fcbff-5d7f-7caa-89e8-953597400088 -->
* **CSS enter transition never fires if data-show is true on first React paint**: Trap: setting \`data-show="true"\` on mount (so a CSS transition can key off the attribute) looks correct, but React mounts the subtree with the attribute already true, so the browser never sees an attribute change and the transition doesn't fire on first paint. Fix used in the voice pill (apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/app.tsx): hold \`data-show="false"\` for one committed frame, then flip it true — guarantees a real transition the browser detects and animates (also enables the right-to-left bar stagger). Applies to any enter-transition gated by a data-attribute set at mount time.

<!-- lore:019fcc1b-9630-795e-9a38-244c6af14bef -->
* **CSS enter transition never fires when React mounts with final state already set**: Trap: adding a CSS enter transition (e.g. \`data-show="true"\` triggering opacity/scale via stylesheet) looks sufficient, but if React's first commit already renders the element with the 'shown' attribute set, there's no attribute change for the browser to transition from — CSS transitions don't fire on first paint. Fix (voice pill PR #541): hold the component in the initial/hidden state for one committed frame, then flip to shown on a subsequent frame/tick so the browser sees an actual attribute transition. Applies to any enter-animation gated by an attribute/class set synchronously at mount.

<!-- lore:019fcc78-812d-7e14-a323-8fe177878b1a -->
* **CSS enter transitions don't fire when data-show is pre-set at mount**: Trap: setting \`data-show="true"\` (or any transition-triggering attribute/class) on a React subtree at initial mount looks like it should animate in, since the CSS transition rule exists — but browsers don't transition on first paint, so the element just appears in its final state with no animation. Fixed in voice-pill motion (apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/app.tsx, PR #541) by holding the false/initial state for one committed frame, then flipping it on a subsequent render so the transition has a starting point to animate from.

<!-- lore:019fcc03-0942-76dc-8a4b-eaf8781be723 -->
* **CSS transitions skip when data-show=true on first mount paint**: Trap: React can mount a component with \`data-show="true"\` already set on the DOM node, so a CSS transition gated on that attribute never fires — it looks correct because the 'right' attribute value is set, but the browser needs two distinct paints (false→true) to detect a state change and animate. Fix: hold the initial render at \`data-show="false"\` for one committed frame, then flip to true, so the transition actually runs. Found/fixed in PR #541 (voice pill arrival animation, apps/electron/src/renderer/src/pages/app.tsx), which also staggers bars right-to-left via an index param in \`BAR\_X\_POSITIONS.map()\`.

<!-- lore:019fd3ad-c703-7fe4-a766-3810c0cae287 -->
* **Custom endpoint discovered models not wired to voice picker rows**: EndpointConnectState.models (apps/electron .../use-endpoint-connect.ts) is populated by runEndpointTest() on a successful /openai-stt/test probe, but voice picker rows are built exclusively from m.voiceItems via buildVoiceRows() in model-list.tsx — the discovered \`models: string\[]\` array is never merged into voiceItems. Looks complete because the UI shows 'Connected · N models found', but there's no row/selection wiring, so users can't pick a specific discovered model; the actually configured voice model stays whatever it was (e.g. openai/whisper-1), just redirected to the custom base URL. Fix: render discovered models as selectable rows; on select, configure \`openai/\<model-id>\` (reuses existing \`openai\` provider's base-URL override, not a new provider) since server/streaming/utils.ts's stripProviderPrefix() strips the \`openai/\` prefix before routing.

<!-- lore:019fd679-2df4-7169-89f9-82e10635dbfc -->
* **Custom endpoint test discovers models but doesn't expose them as selectable rows**: EndpointConnectState.models (set by runEndpointTest in use-endpoint-connect.ts) only feeds "Connected · N models found" text in EndpointConnectForm (model-list.tsx) — it's never merged into voiceItems, which is the sole source buildVoiceRows() reads to render selectable Row objects. Looks complete because the UI gives feedback on model count, but there's no wiring from discovered model IDs to pickable rows: user can save a custom OpenAI-compatible base URL/API key but can't choose a specific model on that endpoint — the active voice model stays whatever it was before (e.g. openai/whisper-1), just redirected to the custom base URL. Fix in progress: extract OpenaiSttConnect into a dedicated 'Custom endpoint' PickerOption (voice only), render each discovered model ID as a selectable row post-Test, and configure openai/\<model-id> on selection.

<!-- lore:019fd6bb-d23f-775c-a040-cbd026f65d6e -->
* **Custom endpoint Test discovers models but never wires them into voiceItems rows**: \`useEndpointConnect\`'s \`runEndpointTest()\` (apps/electron/.../use-endpoint-connect.ts) probes a custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint and sets \`EndpointConnectState.models: string\[]\` on success, then calls \`onDone()\` (loadData refresh) — but nothing merges those discovered model IDs into \`m.voiceItems\`, which is the sole source \`buildVoiceRows()\` (model-list.tsx) uses to build selectable \`Row\[]\`. Looks like a working feature because the UI shows 'Connected · N models found', but there's no path from that list to a pickable row — the active voice model stays whatever it was (e.g. \`openai/whisper-1\`), just silently redirected to the custom base URL via the \`openai\` provider's base-URL override. Planned fix: render discovered models as selectable rows in a new 'Custom endpoint' view; selecting one sets \`openai/\<model-id>\` as the active model.

<!-- lore:019fd756-97c5-7f43-9b9d-58f66a10c605 -->
* **Custom endpoint Test results never become selectable model rows**: In apps/electron models UI, entering a custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint (use-endpoint-connect.ts runEndpointTest) probes /openai-stt/test and shows "Connected · N models found", but the returned \`models: string\[]\` on EndpointConnectState is never merged into \`voiceItems\`/\`buildVoiceRows\` (model-list.tsx) — the only source for pickable Row objects. Looks fixed because the test succeeds and shows a count, but there's no way to actually select a discovered model: the active voice model stays whatever it was (e.g. openai/whisper-1), just redirected to the new base URL. Fix (Aug 2026): render discovered models as selectable PickerOption rows in a new "Custom endpoint" view; selecting one configures \`openai/\<discovered-id>\` (reuses the base-URL-overridden \`openai\` provider via server's stripProviderPrefix).

<!-- lore:019fd70c-f96e-7e55-aae5-fa86088d27da -->
* **Custom OpenAI endpoint models discovered but not selectable (model-list.tsx)**: In apps/electron model-list.tsx/use-endpoint-connect.ts, testing a custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint (OpenaiSttConnect form) calls runEndpointTest(), which persists base\_url/api\_key and sets EndpointConnectState.models: string\[] from the probe result, showing 'Connected · N models found'. Looks like a complete feature since models are fetched and displayed — but that array is never merged into m.voiceItems, and buildVoiceRows() builds selectable Row\[] purely from m.voiceItems, so the user has no way to pick a specific discovered model; the app keeps using the default model id (e.g. openai/whisper-1) redirected to the custom base URL. Fix: render each discovered model as a selectable PickerOption row; selecting one configures \`openai/\<model-id>\` (reuses the existing 'openai' provider's baseURL override via stripProviderPrefix in server streaming/utils.ts).

<!-- lore:019fd68c-b63f-7575-bfc3-525c540c2775 -->
* **Custom OpenAI endpoint models never merge into voiceItems rows**: model-list.tsx's \`buildVoiceRows()\` builds voice picker rows purely from \`m.voiceItems\` (use-models.ts), while \`useEndpointConnect\`'s \`runEndpointTest()\` (use-endpoint-connect.ts) stores discovered model IDs in a separate \`EndpointConnectState.models: string\[]\` from the \`/openai-stt/test\` probe. These two lists are never merged. After successfully testing a custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint, the UI shows 'Connected · N models found' but no selectable row is created for any of them — the active voice model stays whatever it was (e.g. \`openai/whisper-1\`), just silently redirected to the new base URL. Looks correct because base\_url/api\_key ARE persisted and the probe succeeds — the missing piece is converting \`connect.models\` into \`Row\`s fed into \`voiceItems\`. Any new voice model source must land in \`voiceItems\`, not a side-channel connect state, to become pickable.

<!-- lore:019fd66f-af08-7969-9c4e-67c6a98fbc79 -->
* **Custom OpenAI endpoint models never surfaced — voiceItems vs EndpointConnectState.models**: In apps/electron models UI, testing a custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint (OpenaiSttConnect → useEndpointConnect) calls runEndpointTest(), which sets discovered model IDs on \`EndpointConnectState.models: string\[]\`. But \`buildVoiceRows()\` (model-list.tsx) builds all selectable voice rows purely from \`m.voiceItems\` (use-models.ts, derived from buildVoiceItems/buildSettingsVoiceItems over the AvailableModel catalog) — it never reads \`EndpointConnectState.models\`. Looks correct because the connect form does show "Connected · N models found", implying success, but there's no path from that array into pickable rows: users can verify the endpoint works but can never select a specific discovered model. Fix direction: render \`openaiStt.models\` as selectable rows in a new "Custom endpoint" view; selecting one configures \`openai/\<model-id>\` (reuses the existing "openai" provider + baseURL override — \`stripProviderPrefix\` strips the prefix before routing in streaming/utils.ts, so no new provider is needed).

<!-- lore:019fd691-308d-7392-8772-1ebbe752cd7a -->
* **Custom OpenAI endpoint models unselectable — EndpointConnectState.models not merged into voiceItems**: In apps/electron model-list.tsx, testing a custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint (OpenaiSttConnect → useEndpointConnect → runEndpointTest in use-endpoint-connect.ts) sets \`models: string\[]\` on EndpointConnectState after a successful probe (POST /openai-stt/test), but \`buildVoiceRows()\` (model-list.tsx:100) only reads \`m.voiceItems\` (from use-models.ts's useMemo) to build selectable Row objects — the two are unrelated data. Result: UI shows 'Connected · N models found' text but has no way to pick a specific discovered model; the active voice model stays whatever was configured before, just now routed through the new base URL. Looks correct because base\_url/api\_key ARE persisted and used at inference time — model \*selection\* is what's missing. Fix direction: render EndpointConnectState.models as selectable Rows, each configuring \`openai/\<model-id>\`.

<!-- lore:019fd739-2337-701d-9364-09bf39e99a89 -->
* **Custom OpenAI endpoint test discovers models but can't select one**: In apps/electron models UI, testing a custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint (EndpointConnectForm → useEndpointConnect's runEndpointTest, apps/server /openai-stt/test) sets EndpointConnectState.models (string\[] of discovered IDs) and shows 'Connected · N models found', but this array is never merged into voiceItems/Row building (model-list.tsx buildVoiceRows only reads m.voiceItems from useModels). Looks like a UI polish gap but is actually a dead-end: there is no code path turning discovered model IDs into selectable rows, so the active voice model stays whatever was previously configured (e.g. openai/whisper-1), just redirected to the new base URL. Fix direction (per user decision): after successful test, render each discovered model ID as a selectable Row; selecting one configures \`openai/\<model-id>\` (reuses the existing openai provider's base URL override, per stripProviderPrefix behavior in server/streaming/utils.ts:74) rather than introducing a new provider.

<!-- lore:019fd69c-7cbe-722c-ae2d-55088e266ed8 -->

Root cause: \`runEndpointTest()\` (use-endpoint-connect.ts) sets discovered model IDs into \`EndpointConnectState.models\`, but \`buildVoiceRows()\` (model-list.tsx) builds selectable rows purely from \`m.voiceItems\` (useModels hook) — the two are never merged. Looks fine because the connect form does call \`onDone()\`/loadData, but that only refreshes \`voiceItems\` from persisted settings, not from the just-discovered \`models\` array. <!-- lore:019fd6d3-f066-7f10-9e2b-129508bfa9ef -->
* **Custom OpenAI endpoint test never produces selectable model rows**: In apps/electron's models UI, useEndpointConnect's runEndpointTest (use-endpoint-connect.ts) sets EndpointConnectState.models: string\[] from the probe response, but voice model rows are built exclusively by buildVoiceRows() from m.voiceItems (model-list.tsx) — the discovered \`models\` array is never merged into voiceItems. <!-- lore:019fd3a1-d087-7e34-b8da-16c335730170 -->
* **Custom OpenAI endpoint test shows 'N models found' but none selectable**: Trap: testing a custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint (model-list.tsx \`OpenaiSttConnect\`/\`EndpointConnectForm\`) persists base\_url/api\_key and shows 'Connected · N models found', which looks like the feature works since the probe succeeds and models ARE returned. Fix reveals the bug: \`runEndpointTest\` (use-endpoint-connect.ts) sets \`models: string\[]\` on \`EndpointConnectState\`, but this array is never merged into \`voiceItems\` \[\[architecture entry on voiceItems]] — so no selectable rows appear and the active voice model stays whatever was previously configured, just silently redirected to the custom base URL. Root cause: EndpointConnectState is a separate state slice from the row-building pipeline. Planned fix: render discovered models as selectable PickerOption rows; selecting one configures \`openai/\<model-id>\`.

<!-- lore:019fd6be-698b-799e-8ac2-d899edee26a9 -->
* **Custom OpenAI endpoint: discovered models not selectable (voiceItems/buildVoiceRows gap)**: In apps/electron models UI, \`buildVoiceRows()\` (model-list.tsx) builds ALL voice picker rows solely from \`m.voiceItems\` (use-models.ts, derived via \`buildVoiceItems\` from the AvailableModel catalog + local status). \`useEndpointConnect\`'s \`runEndpointTest()\` (use-endpoint-connect.ts), used by the custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint test, sets discovered model IDs on a separate \`EndpointConnectState.models: string\[]\` — used only for the 'Connected · N models found' display text. These never get merged into \`voiceItems\`, so after testing a custom endpoint the user sees 'N models found' but has no row to click — the active voice model stays unchanged (e.g. \`openai/whisper-1\`), just now routed through the base-URL-overridden \`openai\` provider. Looks like a display-only omission but is actually a missing selection path. Fix in progress: render \`EndpointConnectState.models\` as selectable rows configuring \`openai/\<model-id>\`. See related normalizeOpenaiBaseUrl entry \[\[019fd388-094d-7fce-b45d-18121aa392a5]].

<!-- lore:019fd3a9-ad47-7988-8c1b-f894c019531b -->
* **Custom OpenAI STT endpoint discovers models but never exposes them for selection**: In use-endpoint-connect.ts's runEndpointTest(), a successful probe sets EndpointConnectState.models (string\[]) via setModels(result.models ?? \[]), but this array is never merged into voiceItems (built by buildVoiceRows from m.voiceItems, itself from buildVoiceItems() in utils.ts). Looks fixed because the UI shows 'Connected · N models found', implying success, but no code path turns those discovered IDs into selectable ModelRow entries — the active voice model stays whatever it was (e.g. openai/whisper-1), just redirected to the custom base URL. Planned fix (Aug 2026): render discovered models as selectable rows; selecting one configures \`openai/\<model-id>\` (reuses existing \`openai\` provider's base URL override, since streaming/utils.ts's stripProviderPrefix() strips the \`openai/\` prefix before routing).

<!-- lore:019fd750-9d0f-7934-9859-561da9b2e67f -->
* **Custom OpenAI STT endpoint Test never yields selectable models**: EndpointConnectState.models (populated by runEndpointTest via probe → client.api.settings\['openai-stt'].test.$post) only feeds the 'Connected · N models found' text in EndpointConnectForm (model-list.tsx) — it's never merged into m.voiceItems, and buildVoiceRows/ModelRow render purely from voiceItems. So after a successful custom-endpoint test, users can't pick a specific discovered model; the active voice model stays whatever was previously configured (e.g. openai/whisper-1), just silently redirected to the new base URL. Looks correct because the UI reports a model count, implying discovery worked. Fix direction: render each discovered model ID as a selectable row; picking one sets model to \`openai/\<discovered-id>\`, reusing the existing \`openai\` provider's baseURL override (apps/server streaming/openai.ts) since stripProviderPrefix() already routes openai/\* through it correctly.

<!-- lore:019fd6aa-d13b-7ac3-b688-2a1de58241b4 -->
* **Custom OpenAI STT endpoint: discovered models never merge into voiceItems, no way to select one**: Testing a custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint (model-list.tsx \`OpenaiSttConnect\`) calls \`runEndpointTest()\` which sets \`EndpointConnectState.models: string\[]\` on success and shows 'Connected · N models found' — but \`buildVoiceRows()\` (model-list.tsx:100) builds selectable rows purely from \`m.voiceItems\` (use-models.ts), which is never populated from \`openaiStt.models\`. So after a successful test, the user sees 'N models found' but has no rows to pick from — the active voice model stays whatever it was (e.g. openai/whisper-1), just routed through the custom base URL override in openai.ts. Looks correct because 'N models found' implies success, but success only persisted base\_url/api\_key, not model selection. Fix (in progress): surface discovered models as selectable rows that configure \`openai/\<discovered-id>\`.

<!-- lore:019fd6a4-4e20-7d68-a461-cf9244c6770f -->
* **Custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint discovers models but never makes them selectable**: In apps/electron models UI, testing a custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint (\`/openai-stt/test\` via \`useEndpointConnect\`/\`runEndpointTest\` in use-endpoint-connect.ts) persists base\_url/api\_key and sets \`EndpointConnectState.models: string\[]\` from the probe response, showing 'Connected · N models found'. But \`buildVoiceRows()\` (model-list.tsx) builds picker rows purely from \`m.voiceItems\` (use-models.ts), which is never populated from \`EndpointConnectState.models\`. Looks like a lossless connection test, but the discovered model IDs are dead-ended — there is no UI path to pick one, so the actually active model stays whatever was configured before (e.g. \`openai/whisper-1\`), just redirected to the custom base URL. Fix direction (approved by user): render discovered models as selectable rows; picking one configures \`openai/\<discovered-id>\`, reusing the existing \`openai\` provider's base-URL override (server strips the \`openai/\` prefix via \`stripProviderPrefix\` before routing).

<!-- lore:019fd75d-dfa7-7aeb-9918-3c1f376b4bb8 -->
* **Custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint: 'N models found' but nothing selectable**: Testing a custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint (model-list.tsx OpenaiSttConnect + use-endpoint-connect.ts runEndpointTest) saves base\_url/api\_key and shows 'Connected · N models found', but the discovered \`models: string\[]\` on EndpointConnectState is never merged into \`voiceItems\`/rows. \`buildVoiceRows\` (model-list.tsx:100) builds rows purely from \`m.voiceItems\` (useModels.ts), a completely separate pipeline from EndpointConnectState — so the configured voice model stays whatever it was (e.g. openai/whisper-1) redirected to the new base URL, with no way to pick a specific discovered model. Looks like a display-only feature working as intended; it's actually a missing wiring step. <!-- lore:019fd398-c8ee-7c89-ae07-001bdecba051 -->
* **Custom OpenAI-endpoint discovered models never become selectable rows**: In apps/electron model picker, \`EndpointConnectState.models\` (populated by \`runEndpointTest()\` in use-endpoint-connect.ts from the settings \`/openai-stt/test\` or \`/local-llm/test\` route's \`{models: string\[]}\`) is disconnected from the Row list users actually pick from. Voice rows come solely from \`buildVoiceRows(m.voiceItems)\` (model-list.tsx), and \`voiceItems\` is built by \`buildVoiceItems\`/\`buildSettingsVoiceItems\` (utils.ts) — never from \`openaiStt.models\`/\`localLlm.models\`. Testing a custom OpenAI-compatible STT endpoint shows 'Connected · N models found' but the UI never lets you pick one; the test succeeding on real model names makes the gap easy to miss. Only base\_url/api\_key persist — the active model stays e.g. \`openai/whisper-1\` rerouted through the new base URL. Any 'let user select a discovered model' feature must explicitly render \`EndpointConnectState.models\` as rows and bridge into the voiceItems/Row pipeline.

<!-- lore:019fd1f7-9b5a-79d8-8214-67ffb23bf655 -->
* **Dashboard settingsWindow destroyed on close — wipes QueryClient cache**: apps/electron's dashboard window (settingsWindow) is destroyed on close, not hidden like the pill window (mainWindow). No close-interception exists — the only preventDefault() in index.ts is on app quit (line 4527); the 'closed' handler nulls settingsWindow AFTER destruction (lines 1011-1018). Since dashboard.tsx creates \`queryClient = createQueryClient()\` at module top-level (line 45), every destroy+recreate reloads the renderer fresh, producing a brand-new empty QueryClient — even though most mount queries use staleTime=1hr. Reopening therefore refires ~10 GETs cold every time (/api/health, /api/auth/status ×2, /api/settings, /api/plugins, /api/history+stats, /api/history/daily, /api/usage, /api/org/active, /api/org/list, /api/dismissed-notifications). Existing reuse guards (createSettingsWindow/showSettingsWindow checking \`if (settingsWindow)\`) only help while the window is alive, not after a close. Fix (proposed, unapplied): intercept 'close' with preventDefault()+hide(), mirroring mainWindow.

<!-- lore:019fd27e-e220-7952-ae68-386a27942542 -->
* **db.ts has no prepared-statement cache — recompiles SQL every call**: apps/server/src/lib/db.ts has no \`Map\<sql, Statement>\` cache. \`readSetting()\`/\`writeSetting()\`/\`deleteSetting()\` and 100+ other call sites call \`getDb().prepare(sql)\` inline every invocation, recompiling the same SQL repeatedly. \`getDb()\` uses \`DatabaseSync\` from \`node:sqlite\` with WAL mode, \`busy\_timeout=5000\`, \`foreign\_keys=ON\`, \`synchronous=NORMAL\`, and requires \`FREESTYLE\_DB\_PATH\` env (throws if unset). Fix: add a statement cache keyed by SQL string in db.ts. Not yet implemented as of Aug 2026 audit.

<!-- lore:019fd279-6d03-75cd-b974-9aacd755699e -->
* **db.ts has no prepared-statement cache; schema.ts has zero indexes**: Verified via full read of apps/server/src/lib/db.ts and grep for CREATE INDEX in schema.ts (0 matches). getDb() uses node:sqlite DatabaseSync with WAL+busy\_timeout+foreign\_keys+synchronous=NORMAL, but readSetting/writeSetting/deleteSetting and 100+ other call sites do getDb().prepare(sql) inline, recompiling SQL every call — no Map\<sql,Statement> cache exists. No index on transcription\_history(created\_at) (hit by history.ts, history-store.ts) or model\_configs(type,is\_default) (scanned twice per transcription in providers.ts). Real perf gap on the transcription hot path, unfixed as of Aug 2026 audit.

<!-- lore:019fd03f-b1a4-7ff9-8fa3-5408cb99ed9b -->
* **Dead-code claims: verify via knip, not grep (preview-rail.tsx false negative)**: While reviewing PR #544, a grep for "preview-rail\\|PreviewRail" missed the actual consumer import (message-scroller.tsx imports it via a "motion/..." subpath), leading to an incorrect claim that preview-rail.tsx (332 lines) and use-hover-capable.ts (23 lines) were dead code. Running \`knip\` (already configured in this repo, zero unused-files findings) proved both files ARE reachable via pill.tsx → message-scroller.tsx → remix-chat.tsx. Fix: when assessing whether newly-added/vendored code is dead, always run knip's entry-point graph rather than trusting manual grep — grep misses subpath/aliased import variants.

<!-- lore:019fb9b0-1965-7738-995e-c59e34aeca23 -->
* **dev:ios --device fails with cocoapods error due to stale ios/ project**: The \`dev:ios --device\` flow runs \`turbo ios\` → \`expo run:ios --device\` → \`pod install\`. It fails with: \`\`\` \[Xcodeproj] Consistency issue: no parent for object \`FreestyleKeyboard.appex\`: \`Copy Files\`, \`Embed App Extensions\` \`\`\` Root cause: the on-disk \`ios/\` was generated by an older version of \`withKeyboardExtension.js\` that added a duplicate "Embed App Extensions" copy-files phase, causing the same PBXBuildFile to appear in two phases. The current plugin is correct and no longer adds that phase. Fix: regenerate the native project cleanly: \`\`\` npx expo prebuild --clean -p ios \`\`\` Then \`pod install\` succeeds (✔ Installed CocoaPods, 114 pods).
